  prepared as a ratification bundle; §6 is explicitly **not** a prohibition on comparing absolute
  magnitudes.

## Compiled Update — 2026-09-15 (research-intake noninf-20260914): a keep is judged on a vector of workloads, and a cheap screen must earn its admission

**Confidence: verified** for the design reasoning and the recorded instances. The compute budgets quoted
below are the handoff's own ESTIMATES, which P0/P2 calibration will replace.

### The cross-workload keep gate: census, witnessed inertness, and per-workload non-inferiority (2026-09-15, research-intake noninf-20260914)

**The problem is structural.** AutoKernel keeps a patch on **one target's** A/B, and the keep lands on the
**one shared champion** that every production workload runs. The loop optimises a scalar while the champion
is judged on a vector. The recorded instance: champion `732389d6` measured **+7.206% decisive** on
gemma-4-26B dec-b4 and **−1.414% decisive** on Qwen3.8-27B-Q8_0, with the same kernel and opposite signs.
Today's required-source validation runs after the keep has landed, and a `failed` aggregate only withholds
LOO. By the gating test on this page, that makes it an annotation, not a gate.

The design in [`autokernel-cross-workload-keep-gate.md`](../handoffs/active/autokernel-cross-workload-keep-gate.md)
(INF-75) has four parts:

- **Census (§6).** A per-workload record of the kernels each production workload actually fires, across its
  recipe's **shape envelope**. The fired set changes with batch shape: one change measured +17.3% on tg128
  and −1.46% decisive on dec-b8. The census must be callback-free, because a per-node eval callback splits
  the graph and defeats fusion. Such a census would report kernels that production never runs.
- **Leak taxonomy (§5).** A keep can leak into other workloads in four ways:
  1. an unconditioned change;
  2. a discriminator the other workload shares, such as a Q4_K gate reaching another Q4_K_M workload;
  3. shared machinery with no discriminator;
  4. interaction at composition.

  Output identity misses whole classes: `GGML_TINY_SOLO_CLAMP` gated 10 ops while outputs stayed
  bit-identical.
- **Footprint classification (§7).** Each workload is classed INERT, TOUCHED or UNKNOWN, and the rules fail
  closed:
  - **INERT needs a witness.** Either T0 object identity (every DSO the workload loads is byte-identical)
    or T1 differential coverage (no changed body line executed, and predicate outcomes unchanged). A
    static footprint is a prediction and is never enough for INERT.
  - **UNKNOWN ⇒ TOUCHED, always.** A stale census, an opaque hunk or an undeclared edit all count as
    TOUCHED.
  - **Vacuous passes refuse.** A census with too few nodes, a coverage run with zero executed lines, or an
    all-INERT screen with no witnesses is refused.
- **Non-regression gate (§8).** Every TOUCHED non-author workload runs a **non-inferiority** test against
  its own margin `δ_w = k_δ·F_w`. `F_w` is that workload's calibrated floor **in its own unit** (arm,
  session or process). A unit mismatch raises `FloorUnitMismatch` and refuses. The comparator is the parent
  tip under the workload's own production recipe. A speculation recipe needs a serving-class row, because
  llama-bench cannot exercise speculation. The e-process follows Annex K, with a labelled `floor_interim`
  rule until the loop carries one. Rows run cheapest-likely-failure first and stop at the first refusal.
  Rows left unrun are `not_run_after_refusal`, never `passed`.

**Multiplicity inflates false refusals, not false passes — which is why the census pays for the gate.**
The gate passes only if *every* TOUCHED workload rejects its H0. That is an intersection-union test, so the
false-pass rate stays ≤ α with no correction. The cost shows up on the other side. With per-workload power
π, a genuinely harmless keep passes with probability **π^k**: 0.80 / 0.51 / 0.33 for k = 1/3/5 at π=0.8.
Without a census, every keep faces k = 5, so a third of harmless keeps would be refused. Each workload the
screen moves to witnessed-INERT removes one factor of π. At k_δ=1 a row needs ≈41 pairs at power 0.8
(≈68 at 0.95). A shared-machinery keep touching all five other workloads costs ≈5–9 h of arm time. A GPU
device-kernel keep is cleared by T0 on the CPU workloads and leaves about one GPU row (§10). The worst case
is priced on purpose: it gives the planner an incentive to write discriminator-gated patches.

**Integrity precondition.** A footprint computed over the declared paths is meaningless while an undeclared
edit can reach the measured tree (AK-RH-1, recorded on [Autonomous Research](autonomous-research.md)).
Until S3-AKU-15 lands, the footprint is taken over the full dirty set, and any undeclared path makes every
workload UNKNOWN.

### Low-fidelity screens must earn admission, and crashes must be told to a sampler as the worst finite answer (2026-09-15, research-intake noninf-20260914)

**Screen admission.** A cheap screen can rank candidates only if its ranking agrees with the target
instrument's ranking. intake-1394#record (MFTune) defines fidelity perfection as the Spearman correlation
between a setting's ranking and the target setting's ranking. The paper derives **no threshold**. Its
released code drops settings with ρ ≤ 0.39, and low fidelity never makes the final decision. INF-73's
S3-AKU-08 borrows that floor as a **heuristic**, not a derived constant. A mechanism family may use a
staged screen only if two conditions hold, both computed on ≥10 journalled paired screen/serving arms
whose serving effects exceed the serving floor:

- the Spearman correlation is ≥ 0.4;
- the correlation's bootstrap 95% CI lower bound is > 0.

An admitted screen may only rank and seed candidates. Every keep or promote verdict is re-measured on
serving.

**Crash encoding.** intake-1393#record (SLO-Guard) tells a crash to TPE as −∞ with a fixed violation of
100. It tells an SLO miss as the raw sum of overages in ms. A miss more than 100 ms over its cap therefore
ranks **worse** than a crash. The encoding also never fired in the published trials, which contain zero
crashes. AutoPilot's AP-66 takes the opposite rules:

- a crash, OOM or apply error is **strictly worse than any SLO/quality miss**;
- overages are normalised as overage/cap, and the crash violation is set above the maximum possible
  normalised overage (or crashes get their own constraint dimension);
- the sampler is told a **finite** worst-observed objective vector, never −∞, which can produce NaN
  crowding distances in NSGA-II;
- a crash is never told as `TrialState.FAIL`, which hides the region from GA parent selection.

  §8 non-inferiority gate and multiplicity, §10 compute budget.
- [`autokernel-unified-surface-program.md`](../handoffs/active/autokernel-unified-surface-program.md) —

## Evidence durability: a citation is only as good as the checkout it resolves in

Measured 2026-09-15 (epyc-root `cff7484d`; research `d4de5535`, `041ecb1d`, `6575c33c`).

A registry that cites an artifact asserts nothing unless the artifact is **in git**. On 2026-09-15 the
master registry cited 12 artifacts that existed only as **untracked files in one clone**: the durability
gate passed there and failed in every worktree, and a single `git clean` would have destroyed the
evidence behind ratified claims. Two ratification hashes on the DFlash2 challenger were hashing
untracked files — assertions that held in exactly one checkout.

The shape generalises past this one repo:

- **Passing in the main clone is not passing.** A check whose result depends on which checkout runs it
  is measuring the filesystem, not the repository. Verify evidence gates from a worktree.
- **WITHHELD is a third state, and a gate without it lies twice.** Artifacts that cannot be committed
  (third-party PII, licence, size) are recorded hash-and-provenance-only per `MEASUREMENT.md` §5. A
  checker that knows only `OK`/`MISSING` reports a deliberately withheld artifact as lost — and the fix
  is a distinct verdict, never folding it into `OK`: `OK` means *a reader can recompute this hash here*,
  `WITHHELD` means *verification needs the original*.
- **Carry the distillation, not the substrate.** A 22.4 MB campaign was carried at 1.54% — results plus
  the provenance chain, not raw per-question capture, `-lv 3` logs or telemetry that was already reduced
  to four integers. Excluding the bulk also excluded a real third-party-PII surface (upstream authors'
  emails quoted into SWE-bench prompts), so minimality and privacy pointed the same way.
- **Provenance docs must cite their sources or say `UNVERIFIED`.** Writing 11 campaign READMEs from git
  history, registry citations and progress logs **corrected three premises** that had been stated
  confidently in the task that commissioned them: an evidence directory assumed to back a quality
  rejection turned out to hold only speed reruns; a placement directory assumed to be "the fix's
  evidence" was the shared attestation set disambiguated by arm; an "A/B" had one arm that never
  completed. A README with three sourced facts and two `UNVERIFIED` lines is worth more than five
  plausible ones.
- **Never date evidence by file mtime.** In a linked worktree every mtime is the checkout time. Use the
  run-directory timestamp, the run's own `start_utc.txt`, or the commit that added it — same defect class
  as the wiki scanner's mtime-vs-content-hash bug (OBS-13).
- **A gate sees only what it scans.** This one scans the registry, so cited-but-untracked evidence in
  *docs and handoffs* stays invisible: four instances remain open as NIB2-73e. The gate's silence is not
  evidence of durability.

## Compiled Update — 2026-09-23: a worktree's own durability failure can be resolved against the main clone it shares git history with (SW-7)

**Confidence: verified** — landed commit, 72 tests, and a real-repo before/after count (main clone 0 errors == worktree 0 errors).

### Key findings

- **A gate that only worked in one clone was tempting every worktree agent toward a bypass.** A fresh `epyc-inference-research` worktree reported **12 MISSING** citations against **0** in the main clone, purely because the cited `data/`/`artifacts/` trees are gitignored and therefore absent from a fresh worktree checkout — not because the evidence was actually missing. That gap tempted every agent editing the master registry from a worktree to reach for `EPYC_ALLOW_COMMIT_HYGIENE_BYPASS`, which defeats the gate rather than fixing the false read. ([speculative-decoding-mtp-refresh](../handoffs/active/speculative-decoding-mtp-refresh.md) SW-7)
- **The fix generalises this page's own rule** ("passing in the main clone is not passing," above) **one step further: a checkout-dependent gate should resolve against the git history every worktree of one clone already shares, not force every worktree to carry a full copy of gitignored evidence.** `check_evidence_durability.py` (research `3e83ed4f`) now re-resolves a citation absent from the checkout against the repo's git-common-dir main clone (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), applying the **same** durable/readable/non-scratch test used there. If found, the citation reads `PRESENT_IN_MAIN_CLONE` — a WARN the pre-commit hook does not fail on — rather than `MISSING`. A `.WITHHELD.sha256` marker at that location still reads `WITHHELD`; a citation absent everywhere still reads `MISSING`; an unresolvable git layout says so in the `MISSING` hint rather than crashing. 72 tests; the real-repo run confirms parity between the two checkouts.
- **`PRESENT_IN_MAIN_CLONE` is a fourth verdict, not a relabelled pass.** It says *this worktree cannot see the evidence, but the shared history can* — distinct from `OK` (this checkout can recompute the hash itself) and from `WITHHELD` (the evidence is deliberately kept out of git per `MEASUREMENT.md` §5). Collapsing it into either of those would lose information the next agent needs.

### Open questions

- None — SW-7 and its baseline note (SW-8) are both closed.

## Compiled Update — 2026-09-25: Typed Decisions and Long-Horizon Evaluation

### Bounded-decision experiments

A typed decision model should be evaluated as a decision system, not as a JSON generator. Required artifacts are the offered candidate set, the full returned distribution, the selected identifier or abstention, validation and fallback outcomes, and the downstream result. Report exact-match or task accuracy together with proper scoring rules, calibration by confidence band, abstention coverage, option-order sensitivity, cardinality shifts, paraphrase robustness, and out-of-distribution slices. Compare at least three execution paths where feasible: generated structured output, constrained decoder scoring, and a purpose-built encoder or dual encoder.

The 510-pair JEV-as-a-Judge cascade was an offline replay. It produced 92.5% versus 93.1% for the GPT-6 Astra reference, retaining 99.36% of reference accuracy at 56.8% of estimated fallback fees. Those figures do not establish live sequential latency, queueing behavior, or realized billing. A live shadow run must preserve request timing, fallback decisions, errors, and per-item outcomes before the cascade can support an operational claim.

### Long-horizon experiments

Algorithm-engineering and self-improvement results require a frozen task definition, evaluator image, model and configuration, budget, seeds, raw trajectories, failures, patch or artifact digests, and wall-clock and monetary cost. Best-of-N and verifier results must state the candidate generator and oracle ceiling. Generalization claims require task-disjoint or hidden evaluation and matched baselines; a public development score is not a substitute.

New Vidya adapters registered during this intake are prospective write-side contracts. The source row makes future evidence ingestible; prose written before the hook emits no claim tuple and cannot be retroactively promoted into measured evidence.
